# a appendix: moment estimation on sliding windows

in this section, we give the full details of the proofs from section 8.

# a.1 moment estimation for  $p \in [1,2]$

lemma a.1 (constant factor partitions in top level). let  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  in algorithm 5 succeed. then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ ,  $f_p(w) \leq f_p(t_i, m) \leq 2f_p(w)$  and  $\frac{1}{2} f_p(w) \leq f_p(t_{i+1}, m) \leq f_p(w)$ .

proof. observe that in the mergesw subroutine, we only delete a timestamp  $t_{i'}$  if  $\mathcal{a}(t_{i'+2}, t, \eta, \delta) \geq \frac{9}{10} \mathcal{a}(t_{i'}, t, \eta, \delta)$  at some point  $t$ . since  $\eta = \frac{\varepsilon}{1024 \log \frac{1}{\varepsilon}} \leq \frac{1}{1024}$ , we have that conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ ,  $f_p(t_{i'+2}, t) \left( 1 + \frac{1}{1024} \right) \geq \frac{9}{10} \left( 1 - \frac{1}{1024} \right) f_p(t_{i'}, t)$ . thus,  $f_p(t_{i'+2}, t) \geq \frac{7}{8} f_p(t_{i'}, t)$ .

we have either  $t_i = m - w + 1$  and  $t_{i+1} = m - w + 2$  in which case the statement is trivially true or timestamps  $t_i$  and  $t_{i+1}$  at some point  $t \leq m$  must have satisfied  $f_p(t_{i+1}, t) \geq \frac{7}{8} f_p(t_i, t)$  so that  $t_i + 1$  was removed from the set of timestamps. thus,  $f_p(t_{i+1}, t) \geq \frac{7}{8} f_p(t_i, t)$ . by lemma 8.10 for  $p \leq 2$  and the definition of smoothness,  $f_p(t_{i+1}, t) \geq \frac{7}{8} f_p(t_i, t)$  implies  $f_p(t_{i+1}, m) \geq 2f_p(t_i, m)$  for all  $m \geq t$ . since  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ , then  $f_p(t_i, m) \geq f_p(m - w + 1, m) = f_p(w) \geq f_p(t_{i+1}, m)$  and the conclusion follows.

we now show an upper bound on the moment of each subnet whose contribution is estimated by the difference estimator, i.e., the difference estimators are well-defined.

lemma a.2. let  $p \in [1, 2]$  and  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  in algorithm 9 succeed. then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that for each  $j \in [\beta]$  and all  $k$ , either  $t_{i,j,k+1} = t_{i,j,k} + 1$  or  $f_p(t_{i,j,k}, t_{i,j,k+1}) \leq 2^{-j-7} \cdot f_p(w)$ .

proof. suppose  $t_{i,j,k+1} \neq t_{i,j,k} + 1$  and  $i + 1$  is the smallest index such that  $t_{i+1} > m - w + 1$ . then at some time during the stream, the timestamp  $t_{i,j,k} + 1$  must have been removed from the set of timestamps tracked by the algorithm. thus at some time  $t$ ,  $\mathcal{a}(t_{i,j,k}, t_{i,j,k+1}, 1, \delta) \leq 2^{-j-10}$ .  $\mathcal{a}(t_i, t, \eta, \delta)$  so that conditioned on the correctness of the algorithms  $\mathcal{a}$  providing 2-approximations to the respectively quantities,  $\frac{1}{2} \cdot f_p(t_{i,j,k}, t_{i,j,k+1}) \leq 2^{-j-9} f_p(t_i, t)$ . by lemma a.1, we have that  $f_p(t_i, t) \leq 2f_p(w)$ . hence,  $f_p(t_{i,j,k}, t_{i,j,k+1}) \leq 2^{-j-7} \cdot f_p(w)$ .

lemma a.3 (accuracy of difference estimators). let  $p \in [1,2]$  and  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  in algorithm 9 succeed. then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that for each  $j \in [\beta]$  and all  $k$ ,  $\mathrm{sdiffest}(t_{i,j,k-1}, t_{i,j,k}, t, \gamma_j, \eta, \delta)$  gives an additive  $\eta \cdot f_p(t_{i,j,k}:t)$  approximation to  $f_p(t_{i,j,k-1}:t) - f_p(t_{i,j,k}:t)$  with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ .

proof. recall that for a suffix-pivoted difference estimator to be well-defined, we require  $f_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}: t_{i,j,k}) \leq \gamma_{j} \cdot f_{p}(t_{i,j,k} : m)$ . by lemma a.2, we have that  $f_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}, t_{i,j,k}) \leq 2^{-j-7} \cdot f_{p}(w)$ . since  $f_{p}(w) \leq f_{p}(t_{i,j,k} : m)$  and  $\gamma_{j} = 2^{-j+3}$ , then we certainly have  $f_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1} : t_{i,j,k}) \leq \gamma_{j} \cdot f_{p}(t_{i,j,k} : m)$ . hence by the definition of a (suffix-pivoted) difference estimator, we have that  $\mathrm{sdiffest}(t_{i,j,k-1}, t_{i,j,k}, t, \gamma_{j}, \eta, \delta)$  gives an additive  $\eta \cdot f_{p}(t_{i,j,k} : t)$  approximation to  $f_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1} : t) - f_{p}(t_{i,j,k} : t)$  with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ .

lemma a.4 (geometric upper bounds on splitting times). let  $p \in [1,2]$  and  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  in algorithm 9 succeed. then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that for each  $j \in [\beta]$  and each  $k$  that either  $t_{i,j,k+1} = t_{i,j,k} + 1$  or  $f_p(t_{i,j,k},m) - f_p(t_{i,j,k+1},m) \leq 2^{-j/p-2}p \cdot f_p(t_{i,j,k+1},m)$ .

proof. suppose  $t_{i,j,r+1} \neq t_{i,j,r} + 1$ . then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that  $f_p(t_{i,j,k}, t_{i,j,k+1}) \leq 2^{-j-7} \cdot f_p(w)$  by lemma a.2. moreover,  $f_p(w) \leq f_p(t_{i,j,k+1}, m)$ . by the smoothness of  $f_p$  in lemma 8.10,  $f_p(t_{i,j,k}, m) \leq (1 + 2^{-(j-7)/p} p) \cdot f_p(t_{i,j,k+1}, m)$ . hence for  $p \in [1,2]$ , we have  $f_p(t_{i,j,k}, m) - f_p(t_{i,j,k+1}, m) \leq 2^{-j/p-2} p \cdot f_p(t_{i,j,k+1}, m)$ .

lemma a.5 (geometric lower bounds on splitting times). let  $p \in [1,2]$  and  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  in algorithm 9 succeed. then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that for each  $j \in [\beta]$  and each  $k$  that

$$
f _ {p} \left(t _ {i, j, k - 1}, t _ {i, j, k + 1}\right) > 2 ^ {- j - 8} \cdot f _ {p} (w).
$$

proof. note that conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that  $f_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}, t_{i,j,k+1}) \geq \frac{1}{2} \mathcal{a}(t_{i,j,k-1}, t_{i,j,k+1}, 1, \delta)$  and  $2 \cdot \mathcal{a}(t_{i}, m, \eta, \delta) \geq f_{p}(t_{i}, m) \geq \frac{1}{2} \cdot f_{p}$ . since mergesw did not merge the timestamp  $t_{i,j,k}$  then it follows that  $\mathcal{a}(t_{i,j,k-1}, t_{i,j,k+1}, 1, \delta) > 2^{-j-10} \cdot \mathcal{a}(t_{i}, t, \eta, \delta)$ . hence, it follows that  $f_{p}(t_{i,j,k-1}, t_{i,j,k+1}) > 2^{-j-8} \cdot f_{p}(w)$ .

next we bound the number of instances  $k$  of the level  $j$  difference estimators.

lemma a.6 (number of level  $j$  difference estimators). let  $p \in [1,2]$  and  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  in algorithm 9 succeed. then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that for each  $j \in [\beta]$ , that  $k \leq 2^{j + 10}$  for any  $t_{i,j,k}$ .

proof. for a fixed  $j$ , let  $r$  be the number of instances of  $t_{i,j,k}$ . for each  $k \in [r]$ , let  $u_k$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_{i,j,k-1}$  and  $t_{i,j,k}$ . let  $u$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_{i+1}$  and  $t$  and let  $v$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_i$  and  $t$ . then by lemma a.5, we have  $f_p(u_k + u_{k+1}) > 2^{-j-8} \cdot f_p(w)$ . by lemma a.1,  $f_p(t_i, m) \leq 2f_p(w)$ . for  $p \in [1,2]$ , we have  $f_p(t_i, m) \geq \sum_{\ell=1}^{r/2} f_p(u_{2\ell-1} + u_\ell)$ . thus for  $r > 2^{j+10}$  implies that  $f_p(t_i, m) > 2f_p(w)$ , which is a contradiction. similarly for  $p < 1$ , we have that

$$
f _ {p} \left(u + \sum_ {\ell = k} ^ {r} u _ {\ell}\right) - f _ {p} \left(u + \sum_ {\ell = k + 2} ^ {r} u _ {\ell}\right) \geq 2 ^ {- j - 8} \cdot f _ {p} (v).
$$

hence  $r > 2^{j + 10}$  implies

$$
f _ {p} (v) = f _ {p} \left(u + \sum_ {\ell = 1} ^ {r} u _ {\ell}\right) > f _ {p} (v),
$$

which is a contradiction. hence, it follows that  $r \leq 2^{j + 10}$ .

we next bound the number of level  $j$  difference estimators that can occur from the end of the previous level  $j - 1$  difference estimator to the time when the sliding window begins. we say a difference estimator  $\mathcal{c}_{i,j,k}$  is active if  $k \in [a_j, b_j]$  for the indices  $a_j$  and  $b_j$  defined in algorithm 9. the active difference estimators in each level will be the algorithms whose output are subtracted from the initial rough estimate to form the final estimate of  $f_2(w)$ .

lemma a.7 (number of active level  $j$  difference estimators). let  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  in algorithm 9 succeed. for  $p \in [1,2]$  and each  $j \in [\beta]$ , let  $a_j$  be the smallest index such that  $t_{i,j,a_j} \geq c_{j-1}$  and let  $b_j$  be the largest index such that  $t_{i,j,b_j} \leq m - w + 1$ . then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have  $b_j - a_j \leq 512$ .

proof. conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that for each  $j \in [\beta]$  and all  $k$ ,

$$
2 ^ {- j - 8} \cdot f _ {p} (w) <   f _ {p} (t _ {i, j, k}, t _ {i, j, k + 1}) \leq 2 ^ {- j - 7} \cdot f _ {p} (w),
$$

by lemma a.4 and lemma a.5. by lemma a.1,  $f_{p}(t_{i},m) \leq 2f_{p}(w)$ . thus for  $j = 1$ , we have  $j \leq 512$ , so  $b_{j} - a_{j} \leq 1024$ . for  $j > 1$ , note that if  $b_{j} - a_{j} > 1024$ , then we have  $f_{p}(t_{i,j,a_{j}},t_{i,j,b_{j}}) > 2^{-j - 6} \cdot f_{p}(w)$  for  $p \in [1,2]$ . since  $t_{i,j,a_j} = t_{i,j - 1,b_{j - 1}} = c_{j - 1}$  and  $b_{j} \leq m - w + 1$ , then there exists an index  $x > b_{j - 1}$  such that  $t_{i,j - 1,x} > m - w + 1$ , which contradicts the maximality of  $c_{j - 1} = t_{i,j - 1,b_{j - 1}}$ .

lemma a.8 (correctness of sliding window algorithm). for  $p \in [1,2]$ , algorithm 9 outputs a  $(1 + \varepsilon)$ -approximation to  $f_{p}(w)$ .

proof. let  $f$  be the frequency vector induced by the window and let  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$  and let  $c_0 = t_i$ . let  $u$  be the frequency vector induced by the updates of the stream from time  $t_i$  to  $m$ , so that we have  $u \succeq f$  coordinate-wise, since  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . for each  $j \in [\beta]$ , let  $a_j$  be the smallest index such that  $t_{i,j,a_j} \geq c_{i-1}$  and let  $b_j$  be the largest index such that  $t_{i,j,b_j} \leq m - w + 1$ . since mergesw does not merge indices of  $t_{i,j,k}$  that are indices of  $t_{i,j-1,k'}$ , it follows that  $t_{i,j,a_j} = c_{i-1}$ . let  $u_j$  be the frequency vector induced by the updates of the stream from time  $a_j$  to  $b_j$ . let  $v$  denote the frequency vector induced by the updates of the stream from  $c_\beta$  to  $m - w + 1$ . thus, we have  $u = \sum_{j=1}^\beta u_j + v + f$ , so it remains to show that:

(1)  $f_{p}(v + f) - f_{p}(f)\leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot f_{p}(f)$  
(2) we have an additive  $\frac{\varepsilon}{2} \cdot f_p(f)$  approximation to  $f_p(v + f)$ .

combined, these two statements show that we have a multiplicative  $(1 + \varepsilon)$ -approximation of  $f_{p}(f)$ .

to show that  $f_{p}(v + f) - f_{p}(f) \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \cdot f_{p}(f)$ , let  $k$  be the index such that  $t_{i,\beta,k} = c_{\beta}$ , so that  $t_{i,\beta,k} \leq m - w + 1 \leq t_{i,\beta,k+1}$ . note that if  $t_{i,\beta,k} = m - w + 1$  then  $v = 0$  and so the claim is trivially true. otherwise by the definition of the choice of  $c_{\beta}$ , we have that either  $t_{i,\beta,k+1} > m - w + 1$  so that  $t_{i,\beta,k+1} > t_{i,\beta,k+1}$ . by lemma 8.7, it follows that  $f_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k},m) - f_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m) \leq 2^{-\beta/p-2} p \cdot f_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m)$ . since  $\beta = \left\lceil \log \frac{100p}{\varepsilon^p} \right\rceil$ , then it follows that  $f_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k},m) - f_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m) \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{4} \cdot f_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m)$ . by lemma 8.3, we have that  $\frac{\varepsilon}{4} \cdot f_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m) \leq 2f(w) = 2f(f)$ . thus,  $f_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k},m) - f_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m) \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \cdot f_{p}(f)$ . since the updates from the times between  $t_{i,\beta,k}$  and  $m$  form the vector  $v + f$ , we have  $f_{p}(v + f) - f_{p}(t_{i,\beta,k+1},m) \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \cdot f_{p}(f)$ . since  $t_{i,\beta,k+1} > m - w + 1$  then by the monotonicity of  $f_{p}$ , we have that  $f_{p}(v + f) - f_{p}(f) \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \cdot f_{p}(f)$ , as desired.

we next show that we have an additive  $\frac{\varepsilon}{2} \cdot f_p(f)$  approximation to  $f_p(v + f)$ . if we define

$$
\delta_ {j} := f _ {p} \left(u - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {j - 1} u _ {k}\right) - f _ {p} \left(u - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {j} u _ {k}\right),
$$

then we have

$$
\sum_ {j = 1} ^ {\beta} \delta_ {j} = f _ {p} (u) - f _ {p} (v + f).
$$

observe that  $\delta_j$  is approximated by the active level  $j$  difference estimators. by lemma 8.6, there are at most 16 active indices at level  $j$ . since the difference estimators are well-defined by lemma 8.4 and each difference estimator sdiffest uses accuracy parameter  $\eta = \frac{\varepsilon}{1024\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}$ , then the additive error in the estimation of  $f_{p}(v + f)$  incurred by  $y_{j}$  is at most

$$
1 6 \cdot \frac {\varepsilon}{1 0 2 4 \log \frac {1}{\varepsilon}} \cdot f _ {p} (u) = \frac {\varepsilon}{6 4 \log \frac {1}{\varepsilon}} \cdot f _ {p} (u).
$$

summing across all  $\beta \leq \log \frac{16}{\varepsilon^2}$  levels, then the total error in the estimation  $z$  of  $f_{p}(v + f)$  across all levels  $y_{j}$  with  $j \in [\beta]$  is at most

$$
\frac {\varepsilon}{3 2} \cdot f _ {p} (u) \leq \frac {\varepsilon}{1 6} \cdot f _ {p} (f).
$$

thus, we have an additive  $\frac{\varepsilon}{2} \cdot f_p(f)$  approximation to  $f_p(v + f)$  as desired. since  $f_p(v + f) - f_p(f) \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \cdot f_p(f)$ , then algorithm 5 outputs a  $(1 + \varepsilon)$ -approximation to  $f_p(w)$ .

# a.2 moment estimation for  $p \in (0,1]$

for  $p \in (0,1]$ , the algorithm is nearly identical to that of  $p \in [1,2]$ . however, the mergesw subroutine now merges two sketches when the sum of their contributions from their difference estimator is small, rather than the sum of their moments is small. in particular, note the merge condition of algorithm 11 compared to algorithm 14.

algorithm 14 subroutine mergesw of algorithm 5: removes extraneous subroutines  
1: let  $s$  be the number of instances of  $\mathcal{a}$  and  $p\in (0,1]$    
2:  $\beta \gets \left\lceil \log \frac{100}{\varepsilon} \right\rceil$    
3: for  $i\in [s],j\in [\beta ]$  do ▷difference estimator maintenance   
4: let  $r$  be the number of times  $t_{i,j,*}$    
5: for  $k\in [r - 1]$  do ▷merges two algorithms with "small" contributions   
6: if  $p <   1$  and sdiffest  $(t_{i,j,k - 1},t_{i,j,k},t,\gamma_j,\eta ,\delta) + \mathrm{sdiffest}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k + 1},t,\gamma_j,\eta ,\delta)\leq$ $2^{-j - 10}\cdot a_i(t_i,t,\eta ,\delta)$  then   
7: if  $t_{i,j,k}\notin \{t_{i,j - 1,*}\}$  then   
8: merge (add) the sketches for  $\mathcal{a}(t_{i,j,k - 1},t_{i,j,k},1,\delta)$  and  $\mathcal{a}(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k + 1},1,\delta)$    
9: merge (add) the sketches for sdiffest  $(t_{i,j,k - 1},t_{i,j,k},t,\gamma_j,\eta ,\delta)$  and sdiffest  $(t_{i,j,k},t_{i,j,k + 1},t,\gamma_j,\eta ,\delta)$    
10: relabel the times  $t_{i,j,*}$    
11: for  $i\in [s - 2]$  do ▷smooth histogram maintenance   
12: if  $\mathcal{a}(t_{i + 2},t,\eta ,\delta)\geq \frac{9}{10}\mathcal{a}(t_i,t,\eta ,\delta)$  then   
13: for  $j\in [\beta ]$  do   
14: append the times  $t_{i + 1,j,*}$  to  $\{t_{i,j,*}\}$    
15: delete  $t_{i + 1}$  and all times  $t_{i + 1,**}$    
16: relabel the times  $\{t_i\}$  and  $\{t_{i,j,*}\}$

the analysis is mostly the same as the case for  $p \in [1,2]$ . however, rather than bounding the moments of the frequency vectors induced by each block, we instead bound their contributions to the difference estimators.

lemma a.9 (accuracy of difference estimators). let  $p \in (0,1]$  and  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  and sdiffest in algorithm 5 succeed. then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that for each  $j \in [\beta]$  and all  $k$ , sdiffest( $t_{i,j,k-1}, t_{i,j,k}, t, \gamma_j, \eta, \delta$ ) gives an additive  $\eta \cdot f_p(t_{i,j,k}:t)$  approximation to  $f_p(t_{i,j,k-1}:t) - f_p(t_{i,j,k}:t)$ .

proof. recall that for a suffix-pivoted difference estimator to be well-defined, we require  $f_{p}(t_{i,j,k - 1}: t_{i,j,k}) \leq \gamma_{j} \cdot f_{p}(t_{i,j,k}: t)$  or  $f_{p}(t_{i,j,k - 1}: t) - f_{p}(t_{i,j,k - 1}: t_{i,j,k}) \leq \gamma_{j} \cdot f_{p}(t_{i,j,k}: t)$ . observe that at the time  $t$  when the splitting time  $t_{i,j,k}$  is chosen and conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we must have

$$
f _ {p} \left(t _ {i, j, k - 1}: t\right) - f _ {p} \left(t _ {i, j, k}: t\right) \leq 2 ^ {- j - 7} \cdot f _ {p} \left(t _ {i, j, k}: t\right) = 2 ^ {- 1 0} \cdot \gamma_ {j} \cdot f _ {p} \left(t _ {i, j, k}: t\right).
$$

by smoothness of  $f_{p}$  for  $p \in (0,1]$  in lemma 8.10, it follows that

$$
f _ {p} \left(t _ {i, j, k - 1}: t\right) - f _ {p} \left(t _ {i, j, k}: t\right) \leq 2 ^ {- 1 0} \cdot \gamma_ {j} \cdot f _ {p} \left(t _ {i, j, k}: t\right).
$$

thus the conditions for a suffix-pivoted difference estimator hold, so  $\mathrm{sdiffest}(t_{i,j,k-1}, t_{i,j,k}, m, \gamma_j, \eta, \delta)$  gives an additive  $\eta \cdot f_p(t_{i,j,k}: t)$  approximation to  $f_p(t_{i,j,k-1} : m) - f_p(t_{i,j,k} : m)$  by the guarantees of the difference estimator.

lemma a.10 (geometric upper bounds on splitting times). let  $p \in (0, 1]$  and  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  and sdiffest in algorithm 5 succeed. then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that for each  $j \in [\beta]$  and each  $k$  that either  $t_{i,j,k+1} = t_{i,j,k} + 1$  or  $f_p(t_{i,j,k}, t) - f_p(t_{i,j,k+1}, t) \leq 2^{-j-7} \cdot f_p(t_{i,j,k+1}, t)$ .

proof. suppose  $t_{i,j,k+1} \neq t_{i,j,k} + 1$ . then at some time  $x$ , the timestamp  $t_{i,j,k} + 1$  must have been merged by subroutine mergesw with  $p \in (0,1]$ . thus

$$
\operatorname {s d i f f e s t} \left(t _ {i, j, k}, t _ {i, j, k ^ {\prime}}, x, \gamma_ {j}, \eta , \delta\right) + \operatorname {s d i f f e s t} \left(t _ {i, j, k ^ {\prime}}, t _ {i, j, k + 1}, x, \gamma_ {j}, \eta , \delta\right) \leq 2 ^ {- j - 1 0} \cdot \mathcal {a} _ {i} \left(t _ {i}, x, \eta , \delta\right).
$$

let  $u_{1}$  be frequency vector representing the updates from time  $t_{i,j,k}$  to  $t_{i,j,k'} - 1$ ,  $u_{2}$  represent times  $t_{i,j,k'}$  to  $t_{i,j,k+1}$ , and  $u$  represent times  $t_{i,j,k}$  to  $t_{i,j,k+1}$ , so that  $u_{1} + u_{2} = u$ . let  $x_{1}$  represent the updates between time  $t_{i,j,r+1}$  and  $x$  and let  $x_{2}$  represent the updates between time  $x+1$  and  $t$ . since the difference estimators are well-defined by lemma a.9, we have that sdiffest( $t_{i,j,k}, t_{i,j,k'}, x, \gamma_{j}, \eta, \delta$ ) is at most a 2-approximation to  $f_{p}(u_{1} + u_{2} + x_{1}) - f_{p}(u_{2} + x_{1})$  and we similarly obtain a 2-approximation to  $f_{p}(u_{2} + x_{1}) - f_{p}(x_{1})$ . thus,

$$
f _ {p} (u + x _ {1}) - f _ {p} (x _ {1}) = f _ {p} (u _ {1} + u _ {2} + x _ {1}) - f _ {p} (x _ {1}) \leq 2 ^ {- j - 7} \cdot f _ {p} (x _ {1}).
$$

by the smoothness of  $f_{p}$  in lemma 8.10 for  $p \in (0,1]$ , we thus have that for any vector  $x_{2}$

$$
f _ {p} (u + v) - f _ {p} (v) = f _ {p} (u + x _ {1} + x _ {2}) - f _ {p} (x _ {1} + x _ {2}) \leq 2 ^ {- j - 7} \cdot f _ {p} (x _ {1} + x _ {2}) = 2 ^ {- j - 7} \cdot f _ {p} (v).
$$

lemma a.11 (geometric lower bounds on splitting times). let  $p \in (0, 1]$  and  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  and sdiffest in algorithm 5 succeed. let  $u$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_{i,j,r}$  and  $t_{i,j,r+2}$  and let  $w$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_{i,j,r+2}$  and  $t$ . finally, let  $w$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_i$  and  $t$ . then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that

$$
f _ {p} (u + v) - f _ {p} (v) \geq 2 ^ {- j - 8} \cdot f _ {2} (w).
$$

proof. let  $u_{1}$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_{i,j,r}$  and  $t_{i,j,r+1}$  and  $u_{2}$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_{i,j,r+1}$  and  $t_{i,j,r+2}$ . recall that mergesw for  $p \in (0,1]$  merges two instances of sdiffest when

$$
\operatorname {s d i f f e s t} \left(t _ {i, j, k - 1}, t _ {i, j, k}, t, \gamma_ {j}, \eta , \delta\right) + \operatorname {s d i f f e s t} \left(t _ {i, j, k}, t _ {i, j, k + 1}, t, \gamma_ {j}, \eta , \delta\right) \leq 2 ^ {- j - 1 0} \cdot \mathcal {a} _ {i} \left(t _ {i}, t, \eta , \delta\right).
$$

thus we do not remove  $t_{i,j,r+1}$  from the list of timestamps, then we have

$$
\operatorname {s d i f f e s t} \left(t _ {i, j, k - 1}, t _ {i, j, k}, t, \gamma_ {j}, \eta , \delta\right) + \operatorname {s d i f f e s t} \left(t _ {i, j, k}, t _ {i, j, k + 1}, t, \gamma_ {j}, \eta , \delta\right) > 2 ^ {- j - 1 0} \cdot \mathcal {a} _ {i} \left(t _ {i}, t, \eta , \delta\right).
$$

since the difference estimators are well-defined by lemma a.9, then  $\mathrm{sdiffest}(t_{i,j,k}, t_{i,j,k+1}, t, \gamma_j, \eta, \delta)$  is at most a 2-approximation to  $f_p(u_1 + u_2 + v) - f_p(u_2 + v)$  and we similarly obtain a 2-approximation to  $f_p(u_2 + v) - f_p(v)$ . therefore,

$$
f _ {p} (u) - f _ {p} (v) = f _ {p} \left(u _ {1} + u _ {2} + v\right) - f _ {p} (v) \geq 2 ^ {- j - 8} \cdot f _ {p} (w).
$$

![](images/fe10f21c1d9f57f61ec6911ecfca262967d5a32a811b73d9330a92fc76bf0418.jpg)

next we bound the number of instances  $k$  of the level  $j$  difference estimators.

lemma a.12 (number of level  $j$  difference estimators). let  $p \in (0,1]$  and  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  and sdiffest in algorithm 5 succeed. then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that for each  $j \in [\beta]$ , that  $k \leq 2^{j + 10}$  for any  $t_{i,j,k}$ .

proof. let  $r$  be the number of instances of timestamps  $t_{i,j,*}$  for a fixed  $j \in [\beta]$ . for each  $k \in [r]$ , let  $u_k$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_{i,j,k-1}$  and  $t_{i,j,k}$ . let  $u$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_{i+1}$  and  $t$  and let  $v$  denote the frequency vector between times  $t_i$  and  $t$ . then by lemma a.11, we have

$$
f _ {p} \left(u + \sum_ {\ell = k} ^ {r} u _ {\ell}\right) - f _ {p} \left(u + \sum_ {\ell = k + 1} ^ {r} u _ {\ell}\right) \geq 2 ^ {- j - 8} \cdot f _ {p} (v).
$$

thus if  $r > 2^{j + 8}$ , then we have

$$
f _ {p} (v) = f _ {p} \left(u + \sum_ {\ell = 1} ^ {r} u _ {\ell}\right) > f _ {p} (v),
$$

which is a contradiction. hence, it follows that  $r \leq 2^{j + 8}$ .

we next bound the number of level  $j$  difference estimators that can occur from the end of the previous level  $j - 1$  difference estimator to the time when the sliding window begins. we say a difference estimator sdiffest is active if  $k \in [a_j, b_j]$  for the indices  $a_j$  and  $b_j$  defined in algorithm 5. the active difference estimators in each level will be the algorithms whose output are subtracted from the initial rough estimate to form the final estimate of  $f_2(w)$ .

lemma a.13 (number of active level  $j$  difference estimators). let  $i$  be the largest index such that  $t_i \leq m - w + 1$ . let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event that all subroutines  $\mathcal{a}$  and sdiffest in algorithm 5 succeed. for  $p \in (0,1]$  and each  $j \in [\beta]$ , let  $a_j$  be the smallest index such that  $t_{i,j,a_j} \geq c_{j-1}$  and let  $b_j$  be the largest index such that  $t_{i,j,b_j} \leq m - w + 1$ . then conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have  $b_j - a_j \leq 512$ .

proof. conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}$ , we have that for each  $j\in [\beta ]$  and all  $k$ ,

$$
2 ^ {- j - 8} \cdot f _ {p} (w) <   f _ {p} (t _ {i, j, k}, t) - f _ {p} (t _ {i, j, k + 2}, t) \leq 2 ^ {- j - 6} \cdot f _ {p} (w) t _ {i, j, k + 1}, t),
$$

by lemma a.10 and lemma a.11. by lemma 8.3,  $f_{p}(t_{i},m) \leq 2f_{p}(w)$ . thus by a telescoping argument, we have that for  $j = 1$ ,  $a_{j} - b_{j} < 512$ .

for  $j > 1$ , suppose by way of contradiction that  $b_{j} - a_{j} > 512$ . let  $k = b_{j} - a_{j}$  and for  $x \in [k]$ , let  $u_{x}$  be the frequency vector induced by the updates of the stream from time  $a_{j} + x - 1$  to  $a_{j} + x$ . let  $u$  be the frequency vector induced by the updates of the stream from time  $t_{i,j,k}$  to  $m$  and  $v$  be the frequency vector induced by the updates from  $t_{i}$  to  $m$ . by lemma a.11

$$
f _ {p} \left(u + \sum_ {\ell = 1} ^ {k} u _ {\ell}\right) - f _ {p} (u) \geq 8 \cdot 2 ^ {- j - 8} \cdot f _ {p} (v),
$$

since there are at least 8 disjoint pairs of tuples  $(i,j,\ell)$  and  $(i,j,\ell +1)$  if  $k > 512$

thus if  $b_{j} - a_{j} > 512$ , then the sum of the outputs of the active difference estimators at level  $j$  is more than  $8 \cdot 2^{-j-8} \cdot f_{p}(v)$ . in particular since  $t_{i,j,a_{j}} \geq c_{j-1}$ , the sum of the outputs of the active difference estimators at level  $j$  after  $c_{j-1}$  is at least  $8 \cdot 2^{-j-8} \cdot f_{p}(v) = 2^{-j-2} \cdot f_{p}(v)$ . however, by lemma a.11, each difference estimator at level  $j-1$  has output at least  $2^{-j-7} \cdot f_{p}(u) > 8 \cdot 2^{-j-8} \cdot f_{p}(v)$ . specifically, the difference estimator from times  $t_{i,j,c_{j-1}}$  to  $t_{i,j,c_{j}}$  must have output at least  $2^{-j-7} \cdot f_{p}(u) > 8 \cdot 2^{-j-8} \cdot f_{p}(v)$ . therefore, there exists some other  $z > c_{j-1}$  such that  $t_{i,j-1,z} \leq m-w+1$ , which contradicts the maximality of  $c_{j-1}$  at level  $j-1$ .

by applying the same argument as lemma a.8 using the analogous statements in this section, we have:

lemma a.14 (correctness of sliding window algorithm). for  $p \in (0, 1]$ , algorithm 9 outputs a  $(1 + \varepsilon)$ -approximation to  $f_{p}(w)$ .

combined, lemma a.14 and lemma a.8 give lemma 8.11.
